### 🎓 [The Explainer Series: Supermarkets](https://www.slq.qld.gov.au/whats-on/explainer-series-supermarkets)
📆 Thu 24 Sep, 6:30 PM  
📍 State Library of Queensland, Stanley Place, South Bank  
💰 See link  
`history` `technology` `shopping` `culture`

The Explainer Series is about exploring the questions we’re all asking.Supermarkets are part of everyday life, but there’s a lot more happening in the aisles than deciding what’s for dinner. From pricing and loyalty programs to store layouts, online shopping and the growing influence of AI, the way we buy our groceries is changing fast. The Explainer Series: Supermarkets will take a look behind the shelves to explore how Australian supermarkets became the giants they are today - and where they might be heading next. As smaller stores, home delivery, automated fulfillment and personalised shopping reshape the supermarket experience, what do we gain from all that convenience, and what might we be giving up? Join us as we unpack the power and psychology of supermarkets, the technology changing the way we shop, and what the supermarket of the future might look like.

#### [Make Believe: Magic, the Worlds of the Jim Henson Company](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/make-believe-magic-the-worlds-of-the-jim-henson-company)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Buy Tickets
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `film` `design` `performance`

Celebrating 70 years of creativity and innovation, the exclusive-to-Brisbane exhibition ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ will be an unprecedented exploration of world-building and imaginative storytelling. Founded by Jim and Jane Henson in 1955, The Jim Henson Company has created some of the most cherished characters in screen history and continues to revolutionise the art of puppetry for television and film. Their indelible imprint on entertainment, education and popular culture globally is seen in the much-loved series Fraggle Rock and Fraggle Rock: Back to the Rock, the treasured fantasy worlds of The Dark Crystal and Labyrinth, the iconic characters of the Muppets, and the instantly recognisable residents of Sesame Street. In a world-first, ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ will include the Great Hall set from the Emmy Award-winning television series Fraggle Rock: Back to the Rock, taking audiences behind the scenes of a working puppeteer’s film set. Immense and immersive, the exhibition will explore the Company’s dynamic trajectory from practical puppetry to today’s animatronic and digital innovations, as well as its ongoing dedication to the craft of creature design and trailblazing developments in on-screen puppeteering. It will feature hundreds of objects, from practical puppets and animatronic creatures to props, costumes, original drawings and moving image clips. Screening alongside the exhibition throughout its run will be three, free film programs at GOMA’s Australian Cinémathèque as well as a suite of QAGOMA’s renowned public, learning and access programs inviting audiences to explore and engage with the exhibition themes and artworks. This exhibition is organised by the Queensland Art Gallery | Gallery of Modern Art in collaboration with The Jim Henson Company. QAGOMA is excited to be celebrating the opening of ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ in association with Brisbane Festival in September. Works in this exhibition are protected under the Australian Government’s Protection of Cultural Objects on Loan Act 2013. Find out more.

#### [Convergent Bodies: First Leap](https://www.vacantassembly.com/events/convergent-bodies-first-leap)
📆 Sun 20 Sep, 3:00 PM
📍 Vacant Assembly
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `research` `ecology`

Image Credit: Bianca Tainsh (microscopy image with Sandra Tuszynska) Convergent Bodies is a visceral self-identification with other interspecies bodies and the emergence of a speculative posthuman future where technology is applied as ecological steward and interlocutor for entanglement with the more-than-human. In an 11-week OCCUPY residency, artist Bianca Tainsh has been visualising this concept through experimental juxtapositions of a vast exploratory archive of organic artefacts and digital documentation, and an accumulating body of aesthetic and conceptual research exploring microscopic and subterranean realms and the relational and metaphysical entanglement of all species. Unlike Haeckel's Art Forms in Nature , Tainsh's archive seeks the wild asymmetries of decay, sexual manifestation, and symbiosis. #### [The God of Small Things: Faith and Popular Culture](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/the-god-of-small-things-faith-and-popular-culture)
📆 Sat 20 Sep 2025, 10:00 AM – Mon 5 Oct 2026
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`faith` `culture` `art` `history` `free`

'The God of Small Things: Faith and Popular Culture' draws its title from Arundhati Roy's evocative novel to explore the omnipresence of faith in the mundane and extraordinary alike. Centred around a rare collection of embellished oleographs by Raja Ravi Varma (India, 1848-1906), the exhibition delves into the intersection between devotional imagery and popular culture, capturing the divine as a living part of everyday life. Works from the QAGOMA contemporary Asian collection complement and extend this dialogue, through the juxtaposition of religious and vernacular iconographies with contemporary reflections on belief. QAGOMA has embarked on a landmark conservation and research exchange with colleagues in India to document and conserve a rare collection of Ravi Varma works. Specialists from QAGOMA’s conservation team will collaborate with the Museum of Art and Photography (MAP), Bengaluru, to share expertise and deepen cultural ties. QAGOMA’s conservation and research exchange is supported by the Centre for Australia-India Relations. QAGOMA is proud to be a recipient of a Centre for Australia-India Relations Maitri grant.

#### [Sorcerers from Bohemia: Picasso’s Saltimbanques](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/sorcerers-from-bohemia-picassos-saltimbanques)
📆 Sat 14 Feb 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 16 Jan 2028
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`picasso` `printmaking` `art` `history` `free`

'Sorcerers from Bohemia' evokes the free-spirited world of the travelling circus performers, or saltimbanques — the subject of Pablo Picasso’s first major body of work in printmaking. 'La Suite des Saltimbanques' is a series of 15 loosely related etchings and drypoints that mark a transitional moment in the young artist’s personal and artistic development. Created on the cusp of Picasso’s Blue (1901–04) and Rose (1904–06) periods, the works are intimately connected with his paintings and drawings of the same era, including the Gallery’s own La Belle Hollandaise of 1905. On moving to Paris in 1904, 22-year-old Picasso encountered a bohemian crowd of acrobats, jugglers and street performers, whom he sketched as they rehearsed and socialised together. His early depictions focus on the vulnerability and poverty of these itinerant figures, whereas later works pay homage to their strong social bonds and the freedom of their creative lives. Appearing individually and in groups, Picasso’s saltimbanques vary widely in age and fulfil a variety of roles, from clown and friend to mother and king. Ushering in a lifelong experimentation with printmaking, 'La Suite des Saltimbanques' offers extraordinary insights into Picasso’s formative artistic concerns and the social milieu he inhabited at the time. In these sensitive and sympathetic portraits, he conveys the heft of a body, the texture of hair, a playful pose or a tender gesture with remarkable economy and elegance of line, ennobling figures who ordinarily existed on the fringes of society.

#### [Towards a Collapsing World: German Expressionism](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/towards-a-collapsing-world-german-expressionism)
📆 Sat 14 Feb 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 16 Jan 2028
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`expressionism` `art` `history` `printmaking` `free`

The early decades of the twentieth century witnessed an extraordinary moment in German art, as artists responded to a modern world in flux with works that engaged deeply with contemporary society, politics and culture. ‘Towards a Collapsing World’ explores this period of significant cultural flourishing through a survey of works on paper by leading figures of German Expressionism. Rather than a single, unified approach, German Expressionism was characterised by a plurality of attitudes and styles, with artist groups such as Die Brücke (The Bridge) and Der Blaue Reiter (The Blue Rider) forming and dissolving in response to fluctuating ideologies. Common to these disparate voices was a shared belief in the power of art to effect social change. The woodcut print became an important vehicle within German Expressionism, reviving a rich medieval heritage. Its stark, simplified forms — expressed through bold, flat patterns and raw aesthetic intensity —conveyed the inner turmoil and social critique of these expressionist artists with impassioned urgency.

#### [Beyond Henson: Puppetry and World Cinema](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/cinema/program/beyond-henson-puppetry-and-world-cinema)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 12:01 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`film` `puppetry` `cinema` `history` `free`

Inspired by the work of The Jim Henson Company and Jim Henson’s Creature Shop, the film program ‘Beyond Henson: Puppetry and World Cinema’ surveys the rich international history of storytelling through the artforms of cinema and puppetry. Presented in conjunction with the ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ exhibition (12 September 2026 – 18 April 2027), the film program delves into crafted narratives that harness traditions of puppetry and cinema to move beyond belief and into the realms of the imagination. To trace the anatomy of onscreen puppetry is to draw from millennia of performance and craft traditions that extend across the globe. From the marionette puppetry of France and the Bunraku theatre of Japan, to the Wayang Kulit puppetry of Indonesia and the British Punch and Judy show, the democratised nature of the puppet theatre has enabled an unbridled style of performance that can enact the truthful and the taboo. Taking direction from the television series Jim Henson Presents the World of Puppetry 1985, the selected titles include films that precede (and sit adjacent to) productions by The Jim Henson Company, positioning the Company’s work within the broader canon of on-screen puppetry. [sponsorLogos block] QAGOMA acknowledges the generous assistance of the National Film and Sound Archive of Australia, Canberra; the Academy Film Archive, Los Angeles; the British Film Institute, London; the NBCUniversal Archives & Collections, Los Angeles; the Library of Congress, Washington; the National Film Archive, Prague, the Australian Film Television and Radio School, Sydney and the Revival House, Perth in providing materials for this program. Program curated by Victoria Wareham, Australian Cinémathèque with additional film note writing by Rebekah Brammer.

#### [Where the Wild Things Are Made: Jim Henson’s Creature Shop](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/cinema/program/where-the-wild-things-are-made-jim-hensons-creature-shop)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 12:01 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`film` `creatures` `craft` `cinema` `free`

Presented alongside the ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ special exhibition (12 September 2026 – 18 April 2027), ‘Where the Wild Things Are Made: Jim Henson’s Creature Shop’ celebrates the skill and craftsmanship of the artisans at Jim Henson’s Creature Shop. Highlighting the diverse portfolio of expressive characters and creatures made by the Company for external productions, the selected films showcase the work of Jim Henson’s Creature Shop and how it continues to shape the landscape of cinematic storytelling. An important facet of The Jim Henson Company, Jim Henson’s Creature Shop is internationally renowned for creating pioneering techniques that bring extraordinary characters and stories to life. Formally established following the release of Labyrinth in 1986, Jim Henson’s Creature Shop continues to be an enduring creative hub where performance and craftsmanship is paired with experimentation and ground-breaking technical innovation to transform the imaginary into reality. [sponsorLogos block] QAGOMA acknowledges the generous assistance of the National Film and Sound Archive of Australia, Canberra; the Norwegian Film Institute, Oslo and the Revival House, Perth in providing materials for this program. Program curated by Victoria Wareham, Australian Cinémathèque with additional film note writing by Rebekah Brammer.

#### [Justene Williams: Once We Were Mudskippers](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/justene-williams-once-we-were-mudskippers)
📆 Sat 3 Oct 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 7 Mar 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `sculpture` `performance` `free`

Spanning performative videos, sculpture, sound and installation from the past 20 years, including a wealth of new works, ‘Once We Were Mudskippers’ emphasises the aesthetic ambition of Justene Williams’s expansive practice. Like the amphibious fish invoked by the exhibition’s title, which exist between worlds, Williams’s artworks are an amalgam of materials and approaches and remain grounded in art history while reflecting contemporary issues. Adopting a do-it-yourself strategy, Williams fuses disparate textures and concepts into compelling artworks that speak to her belief that our shared future can be forged with resources at hand and transformed by the imagination. Williams’s artworks address ideals of beauty, careerist and consumerist pressures, the strangeness of living in both the physical body and virtual space, and human connections to rituals and the afterlife. ‘Once We Were Mudskippers’ is characterised by Justene Williams’s unwavering conviction that art is a world where artists and audiences can experiment and commune.

#### [Great and Small](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/great-and-small)
📆 Sat 21 Jun 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 23 May 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`animals` `indigenous` `culture` `art` `free`

Animals have played a central part in Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander culture and spiritual beliefs for over 60,000 years. Native fauna form an integral part of relationships with Country, in community and are a foundation of economies, identity and sustainability. Over many millennia of co-existence animals have been central to survival as a food source, for First Nations people they are also absorbed into lore and culture as images of totemic power; and into the ancestral creation stories that link people, land and animals. ‘Great and small’ is a celebration of these connections.

#### [Inscribing a Life](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/inscribing-a-life)
📆 Sat 27 Sep 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 18 Oct 2026
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `history` `memorial` `collection` `free`

‘Inscribing a life’ brings artworks from across the QAGOMA Collection that register existence, histories, and time through the act of mark making. From the brute force recorded in paint on a canvas that expresses strength and vitality, to the time commitment captured in the repetition involved in making intricate forms, the desire to record and share expressions of life is championed in this exhibition. Complementing the adjacent display of ‘Archie Moore: kith and kin’ both presentations share the themes of memorials and cosmic time. Alongside artworks expressing the intensity and wonder of existence are those that celebrate and solemnly honour lives that have come to an end. These artworks speak to the importance of acknowledging loss and how through observing death the public memorial reaffirms the social fabric for the living. The importance of thinking beyond human lifespan can be seen in expressions of the cosmos, 'Inscribing a life' features artworks that grapple with the vast scale and the deep time of the universe.

#### [Geoffrey Burns: Ride the Chronovore](https://www.parkercontemporary.com.au/exhibitions/geoffrey-burns)
📆 Wed 16 Sep, 12:00 PM – Sat 3 Oct
📍 PARKER Contemporary, 38 Hope Street
💰 See link
`contemporary art` `painting` `exhibition` `gallery`

Revisiting locations at different times of year, Geoffrey Burns approaches making as both a method and a measure of time. Works emerge through repeated encounters with place, where observation accumulates and memory takes form through surface and seasonal change. Ride the Chronovore considers time as an active force: consuming, transforming and leaving its evidence behind. Through repetition, Burns traces how surfaces shift, forms appear and recede, and environments register cycles that often unfold beyond human attention. Each return gathers new information while revealing what has altered, disappeared or endured. The fleeting span of a human life meets the slower tempo of the natural world, inviting an attentive and materially grounded way of looking at the quiet force of time. Opening Night Friday September 18 6 - 8pm VIP Preview from 5.30pm Geoffrey Burns in Conversation Sunday September 20 3pm - 4pm + celebratory drinks with the artist until 5pm Artist in Residence Saturday September 26 12pm - 4pm (drop in any time) Morning Musings Workshop Retreat Sunday October 4 10am - 12pm

#### [Capturing Beauty – Solo show by Carol Oh](https://rqas.com.au/event/carol-oh-2026-show/)
📆 Wed 23 Sep – Sun 27 Sep
📍 Petrie Terrace Gallery, 3/162 Petrie Terrace
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `painting`

[vc_row css=”.vc_custom_1523319263370{padding-top: 20px !important;padding-bottom: 30px !important;}”][vc_column][vc_separator color=”custom” border_width=”10″ accent_color=”#4ba686″][vc_column_text]Brisbane-based artist Carol Oh creates works in oil paint and pen that celebrate the timeless beauty of the human form, nature, architecture, and the quiet poetry of everyday life. Her diverse body of work encompasses exquisite floral studies, portraiture, figurative subjects, landscapes, and finely detailed pen drawings of Rome and its enduring architectural splendour. Drawing and painting from a young age, Carol has cultivated a lifelong passion for capturing beauty, atmosphere, and character. Her work reflects a deep appreciation for both the grandeur and subtlety found in the world around us. This exhibition, Capturing Beauty, brings together a collection of portraiture, figurative, floral, and landscape works. Through her art, Carol invites viewers into moments of reflection and connection, creating evocative imagery that lingers in the memory and resonates beyond the canvas. She continues to expand her artistic practice and welcomes commissioned work. Exhibition will be on show at RQAS Brisbane Gallery from Wednesday, 23rd of September to Sunday, 27th of September.[/vc_column_text][vc_separator color=”custom” border_width=”10″ accent_color=”#4ba686″][vc_single_image image=”26108″ img_size=”full”][/vc_column][/vc_row]

#### [Our Communities Textiles Treasures : Textiles Exhibition](https://redlandscoastmuseum.org.au/exhibitions/our-communities-textiles-treasures-textiles-exhibition/)
📆 Thu 1 Oct 2026 – Sun 31 Jan 2027
📍 Redlands Coast Museum
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `history` `textiles` `craft` `culture`

Every textile has a story to tell, and every story helps preserve the history of the Redlands. Community Textile Treasures celebrates a collection of special textile items connected to local families, memories, and milestones. From handmade garments and embroidered linens to treasured keepsakes and heirloom pieces, each item on display carries a unique story waiting to be shared. Among the exhibition are pieces lovingly created for family members, treasured sewing projects, inherited textiles, and items that have become cherished reminders of people, places, and moments in time. Together, they create a rich tapestry of community history, craftsmanship, and personal connections. As you explore the exhibition, discover the fascinating stories woven into every stitch and learn how these treasured objects help tell the story of the Redlands and the people who have called it home.

#### [Audrey Gibbs Art Prize](https://rqas.com.au/event/audrey-gibbs-art-prize/)
📆 Fri 2 Oct – Sun 25 Oct
📍 Royal Queensland Art Society
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `performance` `live music` `music`

[vc_row css=”.vc_custom_1523319263370{padding-top: 20px !important;padding-bottom: 30px !important;}”][vc_column][vc_column_text]Audrey Gibbs joined the Royal Queensland Art Society in 1983 and was recognised as a Fellow of the society in 2009. This exhibition explores genre artworks, one of Audrey’s great pleasures. Sponsored by the Gibbs family, it will also feature a retrospective of some of her works. Genre Artworks are those that depict ordinary people in everyday situations, both in the exterior and the interior. Rather than heroic or mythological subjects, these works celebrate the familiar: everyday people participating in domestic routines, work, leisure, social gatherings, and quiet moments of daily life. Spanning centuries, the show highlights how artists have found beauty, humour, and meaning in the ordinary, offering intimate windows into human experience and social history. Artists are invited to submit artworks in their own choice of media including both 2D and 3D works.[/vc_column_text][vc_separator][/vc_column][/vc_row][vc_row css=”.vc_custom_1523319263370{padding-top: 20px !important;padding-bottom: 30px !important;}”][vc_column width=”1/3″][vc_single_image image=”25724″ img_size=”medium” alignment=”center” onclick=”img_link_large”][/vc_column][vc_column width=”1/3″][vc_single_image image=”25722″ img_size=”medium” alignment=”center” onclick=”img_link_large”][vc_column_text] Examples of Audrey Gibbs work [/vc_column_text][/vc_column][vc_column width=”1/3″][vc_single_image image=”25720″ img_size=”medium” alignment=”center” onclick=”img_link_large”][/vc_column][/vc_row][vc_row][vc_column][vc_single_image image=”26083″ img_size=”full”][vc_column_text] On Saturday, 3rd of October from 2pm , join us at RQAS Brisbane Gallery for the Opening Event and prize presentation for the inaugural Audrey Gibbs Art Prize! For this event nibbles will be provided and drinks available for purchase. There will also be live music! We look forward to seeing you at the gallery for an enjoyable evening of art, nibbles and drinks, and to be the first to hear the prize presentations. Ticket prices: RQAS Brisbane Branch Members: $5 Non-Members: $15 Make sure to secure your ticket via the link below soon as numbers are limited! Please note : This event will be held in the main gallery which is wheelchair accessible.[/vc_column_text][vc_raw_html]JTNDJTIxLS0lMjBBZGQlMjB0aGUlMjBmb2xsb3dpbmclMjB0byUyMHlvdXIlMjB3ZWJzaXRlJTI3cyUyMEhUTUwuJTIwLS0lM0UlMEElM0NzY3JpcHQlMjBzcmMlM0QlMjJodHRwcyUzQSUyRiUyRmV2ZW50cy5odW1hbml0aXguY29tJTJGc2NyaXB0cyUyRndpZGdldHMlMkZpbmxpbmUuanMlMjIlMjB0eXBlJTNEJTIybW9kdWxlJTIyJTNFJTNDJTJGc2NyaXB0JTNFJTBBJTNDaWZyYW1lJTIwZGF0YS1jaGVja291dCUzRCUyMmF1ZHJleS1naWJicy1hcnQtcHJpemUtb3BlbmluZy1ldmVudCUyMiUzRSUzQyUyRmlmcmFtZSUzRQ==[/vc_raw_html][vc_separator][vc_column_text] ABOUT AUDREY GIBBS Audrey Gibbs (1921-2015) was a well- known and greatly loved Queensland artist, whose body of work remains cherished in many collections. She was born in Maryborough and educated at Rockhampton Girls Grammar School. She loved drawing from an early age and was privately tutored during her school years. After matriculating, she worked in a commercial art studio in Brisbane. She married soon after the Second World War and then reared six children. When her fifth child was two, Audrey returned to her art, attending life classes with Melville Haysom. In 1978 she went on to study drawing and watercolour at Seven Hills College of Art. She joined the Pastel Society in 1980 and the Half Dozen Group and Royal Queensland Art Society (RQAS) in 1983. In 2009 Audrey was made a Fellow of the RQAS, in recognition of her significant contribution to RQAS and the arts in Queensland. She exhibited at Ardrossan Gallery (1986 and 1988), Riverhouse Gallery (1993 and 1995), as well as many sell out shows in her own home at Chelmer. Her work became noted for her masterful use of light and tonal values. Audrey’s chief fascination was for figures in interiors, in any medium. Her oil and watercolour painting offer honest and moving depictions of all the things she loved, places she had been, and people she knew. Over the years she embraced various styles in her pursuit of the aesthetic, yet her work has always remained recognisably Audrey. Prizes are to the value of the amount specified above and may include vouchers as part of the award. [/vc_column_text][/vc_column_inner][vc_column_inner width=”1/6″][/vc_column_inner][/vc_row_inner][/vc_tta_section][vc_tta_section title=”ENTRY FEES” tab_id=”entryfees”][vc_column_text] ENTRY FEES [/vc_column_text][vc_separator color=”custom” border_width=”4″ el_width=”10″ accent_color=”#d25846″][vc_row_inner][vc_column_inner width=”1/6″][/vc_column_inner][vc_column_inner width=”2/3″][vc_table vc_table_theme=”classic” allow_html=”1″][bg#d05c49;c#ffffff;b]Entry%20Fee,[bg#d05c49;c#ffffff;b]Description|%2450,Per%20artwork%20for%20non-members|%2430,Per%20artwork%20for%20RQAS%20Brisbane%20Branch%20Members[/vc_table][/vc_column_inner][vc_column_inner width=”1/6″][/vc_column_inner][/vc_row_inner][/vc_tta_section][vc_tta_section title=”TERMS AND CONDITIONS” tab_id=”1607945731535-a081cfe6-1ef9″][vc_column_text] CONDITIONS OF ENTRY ENTRY 1.1. Entry fees are not refundable and non-transferrable. Entry Fees – $50 per artwork – $30 RQAS Brisbane Branch Members 1.2. Entry forms can be completed either: • on the RQAS website, • emailed to: entries@rqas.com.au • or are to be returned to Petrie Terrace Gallery at Unit 3, 162 Petrie Terrace Brisbane. 1.3. The RQAS will email you to confirm if your artwork has been selected as a finalist or not, or if we need further information about your entry. Only finalists will have their work exhibited in the Gallery. 1.4. High Resolution Image(s) of your work must be provided for your entry to be accepted. Images must be submitted as a digital jpeg or png file. Save the file as “yourname_artworktitle”. 1.5. Artists are permitted to enter a maximum of 4 works. MEDIA 2.1. This exhibition accepts both 2D and 3D artworks in the artists choice of medium. 2.2. This exhibition does not accept entries that were completed in whole or in part by Artificial Intelligence (AI). ELIGIBILITY 3.1. Artwork must not have won a prize in any other competition. 3.2. Artwork must not have been a finalist in an RQAS sponsored competition in the 2-year period prior to entries closing. 3.3. Artwork must be for sale. Artists aged 17yrs or under DO NOT have to list their artwork for sale but must notify gallery at time of entry. SIZE 4.1 Artworks must not exceed 1.53m in width, including frame. 4.2 All works must not weigh more than 15kgs and need to be easily and safely carried by 1 to 2 people 4.3 May be made up of multiple panels, providing all panels still fit within the size restrictions noted above. PERMISSIONS & COPYRIGHT 5.1. Exhibitors agree to grant RQAS a non-exclusive, sub-licensable, worldwide, perpetual and irrevocable license to exercise copyright in your artwork for the purposes of administering, promoting and providing information about the exhibition. This includes producing photographs, catalogues and advertising for promotional purposes 5.2. Entries must be the original work of the exhibitor, and not infringe the copyright, moral rights, or other rights of any third party. Entries should also adhere to Indigenous Cultural and Intellectual Property protocols. 5.3. If you are submitting an artwork of a personality, the entrant must have the subject’s consent to enter the artwork in this exhibition including ensuring they understand the artwork may be displayed to the public. PRESENTATION 6.1 The work must be suitably presented and ready to be safely displayed with the appropriate hardware (6.3), as well as being clean and dry. Damaged artworks/frames will not be able to be placed on display. 6.2 Any artwork not clean or professionally finished will not be able to be placed on display. This includes but is not limited to stretched canvas edges being painted in the round or in a solid colour. Stretched canvas must be square. Refer to framing guidelines. 6.3 Framed artworks and works on canvas (or otherwise mounted) must be ready to hang on the wall using large “D” rings placed approximately 5cm from the top of the frame. Wire or string is not required. Refer to framing guidelines. 6.4 Your artwork must be clearly labelled on the back with your name and contact details. 6.5 Artworks will only be displayed at the absolute discretion of the RQAS Brisbane we will not display any artwork deemed unsafe, offensive, or unsuitable. 6.6 Multi-panelled artworks must include a picture of the assembled work with clear instructions for installation. 6.7 Any questions artists have during installation must be addressed to the Director of Gallery Operations or their representative. SELECTION, JUDGING AND PRIZES 7.1. A selection process will be conducted by a qualified selection panel which includes the exhibition judge/s. 7.2. After all submissions have been processed, all artists will be emailed to advise if they have been selected as a finalist or not. Only finalists will have their work exhibited in the exhibition 7.3. Prizes to the value of: – Winner – $5000 – Highly Commended – 2 x $500 prizes – People’s Choice – $250 plus 1-year membership to RQAS Brisbane – Youth Prize (aged 30 or under) – Up to $250 plus 1-year membership to RQAS Brisbane (only awarded if sufficient entries are received to support the category) 7.4. The judge’s decision is final, and no correspondence will be entered into. DELIVERY, INSTALLATION & PICK-UP 8.1 Artists will be notified by email if they are a finalist or not. 8.2 Artworks to be delivered to the Petrie Terrace Gallery on Tuesday, 29th September between 10am – 12pm. If you are not able to deliver at this time, please contact the gallery to make alternative arrangements. 8.3 Entries are accepted, displayed, and returned at the owner’s risk. Freight and insurance are also the owner’s responsibility. All care will be taken, but no responsibility accepted for loss or damage. 8.4 Artworks cannot be delivered by post. A courier can deliver your artwork at the specific delivery times above. 8.5 If you are a finalist and your artwork is not delivered within the times allowed it will not be hung in the exhibition. 8.6 Any questions artists have during installation must be addressed to the Director of Gallery Operations or their representative. 8.7 Artworks must be made available for the full duration of the exhibition until Sunday, 25th October and may only be picked up when the exhibition has finished. 8.8 Pick-up of Artworks will be from Petrie Terrace Gallery on Monday, 26th October between 10am – 12pm. 8.9 Works not collected within three months of the exhibition closing date may be disposed of at the discretion of the RQAS Brisbane Branch Committee. SALES 9.1 Artwork must be for sale. Please allow for RQAS commission and any applicable GST when setting price. 9.2 The gallery will charge 27.5% commission (including GST) on all sales. 9.3 Artists will be paid for any sales after the end of the exhibition OR when artworks have been paid in full. GENERAL 10.1 All entrants and their guests must book into any events associated with the exhibition and purchase a ticket if they wish to attend. Attendance to the exhibition is free outside of ticketed events. 10.2 Should any dispute arise where no specific ruling is laid down the RQAS Brisbane Branch committee’s decision is final. GALLERY HOURS & CONTACT 10am to 3.30pm Wednesday to Sunday. Closed public holidays. Phone: 07 3367 1977 Email: gallery@rqas.com.au www.rqas.com.au[/vc_column_text][/vc_tta_section][vc_tta_section title=”PRESENTATION GUIDELINES” tab_id=”1672956571084-6a92bf6e-a33c”][vc_column_text] Finishing and Professionalism for Artwork Entered into RQAS Exhibitions When entering artwork into RQAS exhibitions, the artworks need to be presented and finished in a professional manner. This helps the gallery to create a cohesive exhibition with a professional look, as well as ensuring the hanging process is easy and safe for our staff and volunteers. PLEASE REFER TO THE EXHIBITION TERMS & CONDITIONS FOR MINIMUM FRAMING REQUIREMENTS. Artworks must be clean, dry and professionally finished before being delivered to the gallery. This includes cleaning the glass and wiping off any dust that may have accumulated while in storage. To download guidelines and view images please click here Painted Edges & Framed Works If works on stretched canvas are not framed the edges of the canvas must be cleanly finished. Artists may choose to paint the edge a single colour such as grey or a colour that complements the artwork, see Figure 1, or you may complete the artwork in the “round” as in Figure 2. In the “round” means to extend the painting around the edge of the canvas. Essentially, the edges should not have random marks, such as fingerprints or paint drips. D Rings Framed artworks and works on canvas (or otherwise mounted) must be ready to hang on the wall using medium or large sized “D” rings placed approximately 5-10cm from the top of the frame. Attach hooks facing upwards and on the outside of the canvas frame, as pictured in Figure 3 . Wire or string is not required. However, if you would like to add wire or string, please see Figure 3 . As you can see the wire is further down the canvas and is not attached to the top “D” ring. Squaring Up To determine if a rectangular or square canvas is “square” or “squared up”, measure the diagonal of the canvas/artwork, and it should be the same length both ways. Please refer to Figure 4. Foxing & Mould Foxing is an age-related process of deterioration that causes spots and browning and can appear as brown, yellow, or red stains on the paper or canvas. If it appears on an artwork mat, the work must be reframed with a new mat. If it appears on the canvas or paper, it must be treated and repainted, so it is not visible. Artwork Labels Clearly mark the artist’s name, contact details, the title of work, and price on an artwork label, to be placed on the back of the work. We need this information to easily identify your artwork before the exhibition labels (didactics) and catalogue are printed for the exhibition. Some artists choose to write this information on the back of the frame/canvas directly. You may also choose to include this on a removable/temporary label, or on a permanent label or “Certificate of Authenticity”.[/vc_column_text][/vc_tta_section][/vc_tta_tabs][vc_separator][vc_column_text] ENTRIES ARE NOW CLOSED. [/vc_column_text][vc_empty_space][/vc_column][/vc_row][vc_row][vc_column][/vc_column][/vc_row][vc_row][vc_column][/vc_column][/vc_row]

#### [Small Figures](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/small-figures)
📆 Wed 20 Sep 2023, 10:00 AM – Tue 26 Jan 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `collection` `whimsical` `digital` `free`

The Collection Online team have been busy photographing thousands of artworks as part of QAGOMA’s Digital Transformation Initiative. This makes the Collection available and accessible to people everywhere, in detail never seen before. With dedicated conservators, registrars, technologists and photographers, the project has provided the resources to delve deep into Collection Storage to uncover and care for artworks not displayed in decades. This exhibition represents a small sample of our teams’ favourite finds – the weird and whimsical, the cute and curious. We gratefully acknowledge the hundreds of donors who generously supported the QAGOMA Foundation’s ‘Unlock the Collection’ campaign to digitise the Collection and make artworks more accessible and discoverable online including this diverse range of artworks, from small figures to small frogs. These are some of the artworks we have recently prepared for photography, and just some of the works which made us smile.

Take a nostalgic journey through the early years of childhood with our Babies & Toddlers exhibition. Featuring beautiful garments, accessories, and treasured keepsakes, this charming display showcases how babies and toddlers were cared for, dressed, and celebrated across generations. A highlight of the exhibition is a beautifully restored baby pram from the Museum’s collection, carefully brought back to life by our dedicated volunteers. Discover the stories behind these special items, reminisce about days gone by, and share your own memories as you explore this heartwarming exhibition.

`art` `science` `history` `family` `kids`

⭐ Dinos Alive is a dinosaur exhibition featuring life-size animated replicas in an immersive Jurassic venue. Walk alongside the massive creatures that roamed our world millions of years ago! 👉 For educational groups (10+ people), other large groups (25+ people) or private corporate events please book here (you can book now and pay later). For birthdays (7+ people), you can directly buy your tickets here Highlights 🌲 Travel back to the Jurassic in a thematic park full of realistic dinosaurs—see these magnificent creatures in their natural habitat! 🦕 Come face to face with +40 specimens of moving replicas: T-rex, Stegosaurus, Ankylosaurus, Velociraptors, and many more 🦈 Dive into the depths of the seas and interact with Jurassic sea giants in a unique virtual aquarium 😮 Learn more about the Triassic, Jurassic and Cretaceous periods in a fun and interactive way 👓 Immerse yourself to the fullest with a captivating VR experience (for an additional fee) 👨‍👩‍👧‍👦 Kids can enjoy a “Budding Experts” interactive space, where they can become palaeontologists and tame the most iconic dinosaurs 🎁 Gift memories to someone special! Purchase a gift card for Dinos Alive: An Immersive Experience here General Info 📅 Dates and times: select your date & time directly in the ticket selector ⏳ Duration: approx. 45-75 minutes 📍 Location: Fever Skygate | Exhibition and Experience Centre, 30-34 The Circuit, Skygate, Brisbane Airport 👤 Age requirement: all ages are welcome! Children under 3 enter free of charge ♿ Accessibility: the venue is wheelchair accessible ❓ Please consult the FAQs of this experience here 💳 ️ Please note we are a cashless venue 🦖 Dinosaurs You’ll Meet at Dinos Alive T-Rex Stegosaurus Ankylosaurus Parasaurolophus Pachycephalosaurus Suchomimus Velociraptor Among many others! Click here to see pictures and read some fun facts about all these species! Description Go back millions of years to a land ruled by giants! This immersive experience in Brisbane brings you face-to-face with the fearsome and spectacular dinosaurs that dominated the animal world for 140 million years. From the turkey-sized Velociraptors to the mighty T-Rex, interact with life-sized, moving animatronics of a large variety of species, and see these magnificent creatures in their natural Jurassic habitat. Step into another world and learn something new in an experience that's fun for the whole family! Get your tickets now for Dinos Alive in Brisbane!

#### [Scott Breton](https://lethbridgegallery.com/exhibition/290)
📆 Fri 25 Sep – Tue 13 Oct
📍 Lethbridge Gallery
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `painting` `sculpture` `drawing` `performance`

Brisbane-based figurative artist Scott Breton combines classical training with a uniquely contemporary vision to explore the living presence of figure, form and space in his work. With a practice informed by deep study of traditional drawing, painting and sculptural media, Breton’s compositions often convey a sense of aliveness and psychological depth, where gesture and structure meet imagination and atmosphere. A recipient of Australia’s prestigious A.M.E. Bale Travelling Scholarship, his work bridges the enduring principles of representational art with fresh insights into the human experience. Breton’s upcoming exhibition offers a new chapter in his ongoing investigation of form, psyche and the poetic potential of the painted surface.

Four quirky misfits turn everyday junk – wheelie bins, kitchen scraps, plastic bags – into an electrifying symphony of beats, rhythm and creativity. This high-energy adventure transforms trash into treasure with explosive drumming, dance and laugh-out-loud comedy. An abandoned kitchen becomes a drum set, plastic bags turn into instruments and the human body becomes a sound machine! It's a thrilling celebration of rhythm, imagination and sustainability for all ages. Speaking in 'Rhythmish' – a playful language made of rhythm and gibberish – this show will hook you from start to finish! Join these musical misfits who reinvigorate forgotten junk. Awards Winner – Critic's Pick Award – Sydney Fringe (2018) Weekly Winner – Best of Kids and Family – Adelaide Fringe (2025) Weekly Winner – Best of Kids and Family – Adelaide Fringe (2026)

📆 Sun 20 Sep, 2:00 PM
📍 Petrie Terrace Gallery, 3/162 Petrie Terrace
💰 See link
`art` `community`

[vc_row css=”.vc_custom_1523319263370{padding-top: 20px !important;padding-bottom: 30px !important;}”][vc_column][vc_column_text] For our next ‘ART TALKS’ event, the Royal Queensland Art Society Brisbane Branch invites its members and the art loving community to: “Beyond The Gallery: Art, Community and Connection” In this presentation and Q&A, Katherine Kemp and Colin Bushell, co-founders of Art & Eats, will examine how they have drawn upon local energy to break down community barriers and build a creative ecosystem, not just an exhibition. They share their successes, the pitfalls and future vision, providing advice on building a model others can follow. Art & Eats delivers the principle of showcasing creativity, whilst bringing together community beyond the gallery. They believe in partnership before ownership and purpose before profit. Katherine is the owner of Ruby Red Jewellery and co-founder of Art & Eats. She is also a Jewellery designer, small-business owner, entrepreneur, valuer, gemologist and MBA recipient. Colin runs Colin Bushell Photography and is co-founder of Art & Eats with Katherine. He is also a multi-award-winning photographer, writer and photojournalist; co-founder and former President of The Gap Creative; former Vice President, Professional Photographers’ Association of Queensland. This talk will be held at RQAS Brisbane Gallery on Sunday, 20th of September from 2pm to 4pm . Afternoon tea is included in the ticket price. Ticket Prices: – $5 for RQAS Brisbane Members – $15 for the Public Don’t miss your chance to attend this month’s art talks event and enjoy the fellowship of other like-minded artists – book your tickets via the link below now! Please note this event will be held in the main gallery space which is wheelchair accessible. 📱 Smartphone tickets ⚡ Instant access to your tickets ⏳ Duration: up to 2 hours 👉 Available in English Dive into the spine-tingling tales of Ipswich Cemetery on a 1.5-hour adventure that's older than Queensland itself! From chilling stories of being Buried Three Times to eerie encounters with the "Headless Angel," get ready to explore Australia's most ghostly graveyard. Led by the legendary haunted-historian Jack Sim, prepare to uncover mysteries like never before - think black magic, unspeakable crimes, and ghostly children leaving their mark. Get your tickets for Goodna Cemetery Haunted History Tour! Highlights 👻 Explore one of Australia's most haunted graveyards. 🕯 Hear chilling tales of black magic and a terrible crime. 👼 Encounter the eerie mystery of the "Headless Angel." What's included? ✔️ A contribution to the Ghost Tours’ Heritage Fund, supporting local heritage initiatives. ✔️ GST (Goods and Services Tax) included for your convenience. ✔️ A 2-hour guided historic ghost walking tour led by an experienced guide, immersing you in chilling tales and mysteries. Additional Information 🐕 Service animals are welcome. 🚫 This experience is not recommended for travelers with spinal injuries, pregnant guests, or those with poor cardiovascular health. 🏋️‍♂️ A moderate level of physical fitness is required for all travelers. 💉 All travelers must be fully vaccinated to participate, unless exempt or under 16. Proof of vaccination or exemption is required at check-in, or entry will be refused without a refund. 👟 Guests must wear flat-soled, enclosed shoes. Thongs, sandals, or high heels will result in declined entry with no refund. 🔦 Each guest must bring their own torch; smartphone torch apps are acceptable. Why choose Fever? ✔️ Millions of users book every month. ✔️ The best prices and availability at all times. ✔️ Thousands of unique experiences. ✔️ Verified user reviews. ✔️ 24/7 customer service. Experience supplied by Historic Australia All ticket sales are final and cannot be refunded, exchanged, or otherwise modified. Click here to check out the T&Cs .

#### [Trash MTG – Pauper Commander](https://www.netherworldarcade.com/event/trash-mtg-pauper-commander-2/)
📆 Mon 21 Sep, 6:30 PM
📍 Netherworld, 186 Brunswick Street
💰 $15.00
`games` `gaming` `drinks`

What the hell is Trash MTG!? It’s a Magic The Gathering event dual hosted by Netherworld and Vault Games,but you only get to use your junky cheap cards. Commander with all commons in the 99. We want you to MINE YOUR CARDS you’d otherwise ignore. The ones sitting in shoe boxes at the bottom of a cupboard, potentially never to see the light of day again…until now. If you don’t have quite enough cards to put something together, we’ll have a collection of 10,000 cards on site you can dig into to trade in and out of. Pauper Commander follows the normal Commander rules except… your Commander is an uncommon, and the rest of the 99 are commons. Health is a hair lower at 30 and commander damage is 16. In the spirit of ‘Trash MTG’ and an encouragement to use what you have instead of riding the meta wave, we’ll have a house rule that regardless of the history of the card, all common cards included must be common according to the card. If you haven’t played Commander before, the Vault Games and Netherworld crew are happy to teach you and will even have a handful of house decks on hand you can utilise if you don’t have the trash resources to pull a deck together. If you’re after any Magic sealed products, make sure you swing over to Vault Games CBD, Clayfield or online via vaultgames.com.au ! Each ticket comes with your first drink and a custom token card. It happens at 6.30 to 10pm. Tickets to the next Netherworld session can be grabbed via www.netherworldarcade.com/events and Vault Games sessions via www.vaultgames.com.au !

📆 Mon 28 Sep, 9:00 AM – Tue 29 Sep
📍 Vacant Assembly
💰 See link
`art` `painting` `drawing` `design` `beginners`

#block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_9720 { --opacity: 100%; --translate-x: 0px; --translate-y: 0px; --rotation: 0deg; --scale-x: 100%; --scale-y: 100%; --skew-x: 0deg; --skew-y: 0deg; --origin-x: 50%; --origin-y: 50%; --block-transform: none; opacity: var(--opacity, 1); transform: var(--block-transform); transform-origin: var(--origin-x) var(--origin-y); } Join multiple Archibald Portrait Prize finalist David Fenoglio for a two-day portrait painting workshop. The workshop will focus on natural and realistic representation of the live model in oils. Limited to 8 places. 9am - 3:30pm both days. An alla prima approach will be demonstrated, including blocking in shapes, proportion, anatomy, design, value/colour relationships, colour mixing and paint handling. Foundational principles will be discussed while demonstrating a variety of technical approaches. Day one will focus on anatomy and proportion with students creating a block-in drawing and one short alla prima painting from the live model. Day two will focus on modelling form, colour mixing and paint application with students creating one longer alla prima painting from a live model. #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e { } #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e .sqs-html-content { } #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e { mix-blend-mode: var(--tweak-text-block-blend ); border-radius: var(--tweak-text-block-radius); } @media screen and (max-width: 767px) { #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e { } } @media screen and (max-width: 767px) { #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e .sqs-html-content { } } @media screen and (max-width: 767px) { } #block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_11920 { --opacity: 100%; --translate-x: 0px; --translate-y: 0px; --rotation: 0deg; --scale-x: 100%; --scale-y: 100%; --skew-x: 0deg; --skew-y: 0deg; --origin-x: 50%; --origin-y: 50%; --block-transform: none; opacity: var(--opacity, 1); transform: var(--block-transform); transform-origin: var(--origin-x) var(--origin-y); } LEARN MORE #block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_12294 { --sqs-block-content-flex: 0; } #block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_12294 { --opacity: 100%; --translate-x: 0px; --translate-y: 0px; --rotation: 0deg; --scale-x: 100%; --scale-y: 100%; --skew-x: 0deg; --skew-y: 0deg; --origin-x: 50%; --origin-y: 50%; --block-transform: none; opacity: var(--opacity, 1); transform: var(--block-transform); transform-origin: var(--origin-x) var(--origin-y); }

Get ready to live the impossible! Highlights 🚀 Dive into the world’s most advanced team-based virtual adventure and experience a total reality transformation 💥 Move freely through your own dedicated space—walk, grab, throw, and high-five your teammates in full 3D immersion 🎮 No motion sickness, no limits—just pure adrenaline and cutting-edge comfort 🌌 Team up to solve epic puzzles, overcome challenges, and celebrate your victories together in real time 🎬 Feel the rush of cinematic-quality visuals and heart-pounding sound that blur the line between the game and reality 👥 Perfect for team building , adult birthdays , kids’ birthdays & hen’s parties . General Info 📅 Date: Select your date directly in the ticket selector 🕒 Time: hourly from 10 a.m. – 10 p.m. ⏳ Duration: 40-50 minutes 📍 Location: Virtual Room Brisbane (Edward St, CBD) 👤 Age requirement: - Time Travel Chapter 1: 8+ (When played with an adult) - Time Travel Chapter 2: 10+ - Press Start: 12+ - Are We Dead?: 14+ - Asterix: Mission Potions!: 8+ (When played with an adult) 📱 For last-minute bookings (within 2 hours of your visit), please call +61 4 2250 0206 👥 Please note: 2-4 players required per game. If you book for more than 4 people, your group will be split into multiple teams playing simultaneously. 📎 You can check the terms and conditions for this event here ❓ You can check the frequently asked questions and answers here 🎁 Give the gift of adventure! Slip on your gear and travel through time—from ancient Egypt to the moon landing—to recover lost intel and save humanity before it’s too late. 🌍 Time Travel Chapter 2 The planet is under threat once again. Track down rare energy sources scattered across time—explore Aztec temples, dive into sunken pirate ships, and race against the clock to save Earth! 🕹️ Press Start You thought you were playing the game… until you became part of it! Enter a retro arcade world and face four pulse-pounding challenges that blend nostalgia and next-gen VR magic. 🧟 Are We Dead? The tables have turned! As the last surviving Zombies, it’s up to you to stop the scientists from wiping out your kind. Form your undead team and fight for Zombie survival in this epic twist of fate! ✨ Asterix: Mission Potions! The village is in danger! } } ⚖️ Watch drama | 🗳️ Cast a vote | 🕵️ Crack the case ⭐ You've been called for jury duty. Step into a thrilling courtroom drama where the audience is the jury. Listen to testimonies, analyze evidence, and ultimately decide… guilty or not guilty? It's not theatre you watch. It's justice you deliver. The Million Dollar Murder Inspired by true events, this gripping courtroom drama pulls you into a world where greed, passion, and revenge collide — and where the line between victim and villain is razor-thin. The accused stands in the dock, but is this a cold-blooded killer, or a pawn in a far more dangerous game? No DNA evidence. A tangled web of desire and betrayal. And a divorce worth millions. Every witness has a reason to lie. Every motive points somewhere different. Can you untangle the truth before the verdict is cast? 🚩 This experience includes themes of murder and violent death, domestic violence, emotional manipulation. There is a detailed discussion of a homicide. The performance contains moments of intense emotional conflict. Highlights 🤩 Enjoy a live theatre courtroom drama where you decide the final verdict 🫵 Vote during key moments - your decisions shape hidden angles in the case, making every show different! 🕵️‍♂️ Dissect witness testimonies, unpack evidence, and weigh every argument 🎢 A high-stakes case full of twists - more turns than a rollercoaster, and a verdict only you can deliver How it works 1 Take your seat Arrive 30 min early. Doors close at show time — late entry is not permitted. 2 Hear the case Track every testimony, spot the contradictions, and vote at key moments to steer the trial. 3 Deliver your verdict The jury speaks. Guilty or not guilty - the outcome rests entirely with you. Practical info ⏳ Duration 60 minutes Doors open 30 min before 📍 Venue Old Museum Brisbane 480 Gregory Terrace, Bowen Hills, QLD 4006, Australia 👤 Age 12+ Under 16s must be with an adult ♿ Accessibility Wheelchair accessible Wheelchair zone available ✨ Groups & Private Take the stand 30+ people or private show 🎁 Gift card Send gift Gift memories to someone special Frequently asked questions About the experience What is The Jury Experience and what can I expect? The Jury Experience is an immersive courtroom drama where you become part of the story as a juror. Analyze testimonies, cross-examinations, and evidence to uncover the truth - your vote determines the accused's fate. How long does the experience last? The experience lasts approximately 1 hour. Will there be strobe lights? No, there will not be any strobe lights. Are seats numbered? No, seats are not assigned. Seating is first-come, first-served within the zone you select when purchasing your ticket. About your tickets What are the different types of tickets available? A reserve: Best visibility B reserve: Good visibility, located on the sides C reserve: Fair visibility, positioned further back D reserve: Fair visibility, positioned further back behind C reserve Can I buy a ticket on site on the day of the event? Tickets cannot be purchased on site. All tickets must be purchased via the app or website (feverup.com) before arriving at the event. Is it necessary to buy a ticket for my carer? Guests requiring assistance are entitled to one free pass for their personal assistant/support worker. Contact us to receive a voucher for a free carer ticket (based on availability). The regular ticket must be shown together with the carer ticket at entry. Can I change the date of my ticket? Are refunds allowed? Subject to availability, date/time changes are permitted up to 48 hours before the start time. Tickets are non-refundable except as required under Australian Consumer Law. Can I transfer my tickets to another person? Yes, tickets can be transferred to any other Fever account at no additional cost via "Transfer tickets" in the Fever app. Where can I find my ticket? Do I need to print it out? Your ticket is in the "Tickets" section of the Fever app, and also in your purchase confirmation email. You do not need to print it - it's validated via the app on arrival. Access & venue Where does the show take place? The show takes place at The Old Museum, 480 Gregory Terrace, Bowen Hills QLD 4006, Australia. Does the experience take place indoors or outdoors? This experience takes place indoors in a heated/air-conditioned venue. Are food and drinks available on site for purchase? No, food and drinks are not available for purchase on site. Are animals allowed? Animals, except assistance animals, are not allowed. Is the venue accessible? Yes, the event is held in an accessible venue. Accessible seating is limited to Zone A and B only. Are there chairs available? Can I be seated during the experience? Yes, this is a seated experience - seating is available for every guest. Will toilet facilities be available on site? Yes, the venue has toilets; on-site staff can direct you. Is there parking on site? There is no dedicated venue parking, but surrounding parking garages and limited street parking are available. Public transport or sustainable alternatives are recommended. Is cloakroom service available? Unfortunately, this location does not have a cloakroom service. Can I take photos or use my phone during the performance? Photos and videos can only be taken during the curtain call, without flash. Phones must be in silent mode throughout the performance. Can I leave during the performance? What items are prohibited? Out of respect for the performers and the audience, you may not leave your seat except in an emergency. Prohibited items include smoking/vaping, external alcohol, hot food or drinks, strong-smelling food or glass, audio recording devices, drugs, flammables, torches, lasers, professional cameras, promotional items, and selfie sticks/tripods. Seating Map Murder #1 · The Million Dollar Murder

Aboard the boat, the sights will be accompanied by informative commentary about the region as well as a morning tea to satisfy your on board cravings. The morning cruise will last for 45 minutes until you reach your next point of call; the Breakfast Creek Hotel. The Breakfast Creek Hotel is a popular hotspot for locals and tourists alike and after a short walk from the landing dock, it is where you can enjoy a delicious meal. Plating up a range of dishes such as Rump steak, Panko Calamari and Caesar Salad, it will be hard to leave the Hotel with a rumbling stomach. From here you will once again board the MV Neptune to cruise back to the dock at South Bank within the luscious Parklands.

`art` `science` `history` `family` `kids`

⭐ Dinos Alive is a dinosaur exhibition featuring life-size animated replicas in an immersive Jurassic venue. Walk alongside the massive creatures that roamed our world millions of years ago! Tickets for Birthday Celebrations at Dinos Alive 🎫 Birthday ticket - includes entry to the experience and birthday room for the celebration 🎫 VIP Birthday ticket - includes entry to the experience and birthday room for the celebration, virtual reality and a Dinos Alive poster * Please always select the 'Birthday Room' option when booking so that we can ensure that the room is reserved exclusively for your celebration. ** A minimum of 7 tickets required for a birthday booking. Highlights 🌲 Travel back to the Jurassic in a thematic park full of realistic dinosaurs—see these magnificent creatures in their natural habitat! 🦕 Come face to face with 40+ specimens of moving replicas: T-rex, Stegosaurus, Ankylosaurus, Velociraptors, and many more 🦈 Dive into the depths of the seas and interact with Jurassic sea giants in a unique virtual aquarium 😮 Learn more about the Triassic, Jurassic and Cretaceous periods in a fun and interactive way 👓 Immerse yourself to the fullest with a captivating VR experience (for an additional fee) 👨‍👩‍👧‍👦 Kids can enjoy a “Budding Experts” interactive space, where they can become palaeontologists and tame the most iconic dinosaurs General Info 📅 Dates and times: select your date & time directly in the ticket selector ⏳ Duration: approx. 60-90 minutes. You will have the room booked for you for 30 minutes 📍 Location: Fever Skygate | Exhibition and Experience Centre, 30-34 The Circuit, Skygate, Brisbane Airport 👤 Age requirement: all ages are welcome! ♿ Accessibility: the venue is wheelchair accessible ❓ Please consult the FAQs of this experience here ❓ Please contact us at dinos.brisbane@exhibitionhub.com if you have any questions Birthday Celebrations ⏳ You will have access to the Birthday Room for 30 minutes 🎂 2 soft drinks per guest are included in the birthday package 👥 Please note that any person entering the exhibition or the birthday room needs a valid ticket 👥 If you are unsure about the number of attendees, please select the minimum; you can add more attendees upon arrival ⚠ Tickets are not refundable Description Go back millions of years to a land ruled by giants! This immersive experience in Brisbane brings you face-to-face with the fearsome and spectacular dinosaurs that dominated the animal world for 140 million years. From the turkey-sized Velociraptors to the mighty T-Rex, interact with life-sized, moving animatronics of a large variety of species, and see these magnificent creatures in their natural Jurassic habitat. Step into another world and learn something new in an experience that's fun for the whole family! Get your tickets now to celebrate your birthday with us!

#### [Little Monsters](https://www.netherworldarcade.com/event/little-monsters-6/)
📆 Sun 20 Sep, 9:30 AM
📍 Netherworld, 186 Brunswick Street
💰 $10.00
`kids` `family` `games` `gaming`

Little Monsters is a ticketed event. A ticket must be bought for each adult and child attending. Each $10 ticket comes with a bag of 20 Netherworld tokens to be used during the session. Extra tokens can be purchased as normal. These sessions are suitable for small family groups with children 12 years and under. We’re often asked “Can I bring my tiny terror?” Our underworld policy is that because it can get a little gnarlier as the evening goes on, we don’t allow little ones after 5pm. There’s creatures stuck in the wall that’ll swallow them whole if you’re not careful. To even that out, we host ‘Little Monsters’ from 9.30am to 11.30am – a morning for guardians and monsters to be. The bar will be stocked up with soda, juice and something with less sugar because we’re not all monsters.Please note Netherworld will be closed between 11.30am and Midday. A Little Monsters FAQ Unfortunately during these sessions we cannot cater to large gatherings or parties, as the sessions are more focused on providing a more relaxed space outside of our regular hours for families to enjoy the games together. We don’t do any table bookings during these session – it’s very casual. The session runs from 9.30am to 11.30am, with a 15 minute heads up at 11.15am. We require the venue to be vacated at this time so that we can clean up and refresh the venue for our lunchtime open at midday.No outside food or drink is allowed. If your child has any dietry needs during this time that requires you bring your own food, this is of course perfectly fine. If you have any questions whatsoever, get in touch via info@netherworldarcade.com.

#### [DEAD HEAD REDEMPTION – Album Launch](https://thecaveinn.net/events/dead-head-redemption-album-launch/)
📆 Sun 20 Sep, 4:00 AM
📍 The Cave Inn, 47 Balaclava St, Woolloongabba, Qld, 4102, 47 Balaclava St, Woolloongabba, QLD, Australia
💰 AUD 0
`live music` `music` `concert` `band` `blues` `country`

It's been a hard road but this wagon train has finally pulled up! DEAD HEAD REDEMPTION are proud to launch their debut album THE WENDIGO HUNTERS here at THE CAVE INN on Saturday September 19th 2026. With support from fellow 4017-ers IRESON ROAD and THE COASTAL COWGIRLS, this will be a night of Hickory-smoked entertainment that's sure to tickle the nostalgia buds. NZ brother and tireless publican Adam Christy, of Cardigan Bar and State of Appalachia fame, brings his new project IRESON ROAD to their debut at the Cave. From gritty blues to powerful dark country ballads, we are set for an emotional ride into the sunset, accompanied by wailing guitar and heartbreaking vocals. Playful, talented, and gosh-darn rootin'-tootin', Sandgate's THE COASTAL COWGIRLS are a female-led bayside band that blend the dusty trails of country & Americana with the refreshing breeze of the coast. Delightful originals, river-washed classics, and yee-haw surprises are these boot-stomping blonde's buttercake. Closing the evening, DEAD HEAD REDEMPTION perform their brand-new album The Wendigo Hunters in full from start to finish. Evolved over the past three years, the songs evoke the atmosphere of the mangroves, the dankness of the swamp, and the harshness of the open land. Weaving tales of folklore, injustice, and murder, the songs tramp the trail between blues, outlaw, and pure rock n' roll. Following the closing track, they'll bring the night to a head with more originals, and a few rusty rail reworks for good measure! What is Vermilion High? Vermilion High is a program initiated by QUT’s student-run record label Vermilion Records. The program is the first of its kind in the country and offers a few talented high school musicians the chance to participate in a collaborative production process with final-year creative arts undergraduates from QUT. For final-year QUT students, at the brink of emerging into their own professional careers, they are able to refine their skills in the music business through production, marketing, artist liaising, and event management, to name just a few. For the Vermilion High students, these lucky young artists gain the opportunity to professionally record their original music at a state-of-the-art facility. It's a once-in-a-lifetime opportunity, and the Vermilion High program empowers these emerging artists to showcase their talent to the real world. The artists performing include: Evie May Braden Judge Sophie Nicholls Kira Green Liv Welsh Sabrina Rees Seb Tyne Prom In The Woods Glass Rain Ethan Dahl Asher Iyer Ruby Elizabeth [button link="https://pay.qut.edu.au/FCIEDUSJCONFERENECES/booking?UDS_ACTION=S1I&UDS_ACTION_DATA=FVxWcjdAXQROXVECNEJDBS1SQnRBXUQbVTJALARBUDEBRllS"] Tickets[/button] Vermilion Records - https://www.instagram.com/vermilion.records?utm_source=ig_web_button_share_sheet&igsi=ZDNlZDc0MzIxNw== Vermilion High - https://www.instagram.com/vermilion.high?utm_source=ig_web_button_share_sheet&igsi=ZDNlZDc0MzIxNw==

#### [Ancient Channels- Static Spirit Album Launch](https://thecaveinn.net/events/ancient-channels-static-spirit-album-launch/)
📆 Sat 3 Oct, 4:00 AM
📍 The Cave Inn, 47 Balaclava St, Woolloongabba, Qld, 4102, 47 Balaclava St, Woolloongabba, QLD, Australia
💰 AUD 0
`live music` `music` `concert` `band` `punk`

Ancient Channels is a Brisbane post-punk outfit known for its swirling guitar landscapes, dream-pop sensibilities, and a cinematic approach to songwriting. The project channels a love of ’80s post punk, modern psych-pop, and atmospheric post-punk into a sound that feels expansive yet intimate. Formed against the backdrop of Brisbane’s tightly knit independent music community, Ancient Channels quickly stood out for their ability to fuse shimmering textures with grounded, emotionally honest lyricism. Their songs unfold like scenes with rich layers of reverb, swirling effects, and melodic guitar interplay creating a sonic world that invites listeners in rather than overwhelms.The result is music that feels both nostalgic and exploratory: echoes of The Cure and Siouxsie And The Banshees woven into something distinctly their own. Onstage, Ancient Channels delivers immersive performances marked by dynamic energy and detailed soundscapes. The band’s live sets move fluidly from atmospheric introspection to powerful crescendos, showcasing a versatility that resonates with audiences across Brisbane’s venues and festivals. With each release, Ancient Channels continues to refine and expand their sonic palette, building a reputation as one of Brisbane’s most compelling underground acts.
